Qregion was observed red at unknown recipe before serialization. Semantic
and coherent tests include false effective controls: images still execute
unconditionally, while body gates are predicated. Empty/default-zero images
also work for non-byte-aligned cell widths, as §4.6 requires. Sparse exact
columns avoid the dense checker's 20-coordinate workspace cap; the output
interface cap remains explicit. They do not discard unused allocation bits.

A capture bug was observed red during the template audit: legacy integer
formals imported apply-site wires over captured free names. Template apply
now imports only explicit formals and shadows captured bindings across all
types; a wire formal cannot accidentally retain a captured integer of the
same name. The regression is in ref_completion.rs. K22's normative lexical
rule already required this behavior. K21's default-zero case was clarified
in §11.1 and the raw diff regenerated. Qxor witness bytes remain unchanged.

# 🚨 SESSION CLOSE PROTOCOL 🚨

**CRITICAL**: Before saying "done" or "complete", you MUST run this checklist:

```
[ ] 1. bd close <id1> <id2> ...   (close completed issues)
[ ] 2. run quality gates        (tests, linters, builds when relevant)
[ ] 3. git status               (check what changed)
[ ] 4. follow active profile    (conservative: report handoff; team-maintainer: commit/sync/push if enabled)
```

**Policy:** Conservative is the default. Commit, sync, or push only when the active user, orchestrator, or repository profile grants that authority.

## Core Rules
- **Default**: Use beads for ALL task tracking (`bd create`, `bd ready`, `bd close`)
- **Prohibited**: Do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown files for task tracking
- **Workflow**: Create beads issue BEFORE writing code, mark in_progress when starting
- **Memory**: Use `bd remember "insight"` for persistent knowledge across sessions. Do NOT use MEMORY.md files — they fragment across accounts. Search with `bd memories <keyword>`.
- Persistence you don't need beats lost context
- Default: do not commit, push, or run dolt remote sync without explicit authority. Team-maintainer behavior is opt-in and still subordinate to user/orchestrator instructions.
- Git workflow: conservative by default; commit/push only with explicit user/orchestrator or team-maintainer authority
- Session management: check `bd ready` for available work

## Essential Commands

### Finding Work
- `bd ready` - Show issues ready to work (no blockers)
- `bd list --status=open` - All open issues
- `bd list --status=in_progress` - Your active work
- `bd show <id>` - Detailed issue view with dependencies

### Creating & Updating
- `bd create --title="Summary of this issue" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task|bug|feature --priority=2` - New issue
  - Priority: 0-4 or P0-P4 (0=critical, 2=medium, 4=backlog). NOT "high"/"medium"/"low"
- `bd create ... --parent=<id>` - Hierarchical child (task under epic, subtask under task; inherits parent labels)
- `bd update <id> --claim` - Claim work
- `bd unclaim <id>` - Release stuck issue (agent crashed)
- `bd update <id> --assignee=username` - Assign to someone
- `bd update <id> --if-assignee=<expected> --assignee=<new>` - Atomic reassign: applies only if the assignee still matches (--if-status=<expected> guards status; --if-assignee='' requires unassigned). Mismatch exits non-zero with nothing written — never retry blindly
- `bd update <id> --title/--description/--notes/--design` - Update fields inline
- `bd close <id>` - Mark complete
- `bd close <id1> <id2> ...` - Close multiple issues at once (more efficient)
- `bd close <id> --reason="explanation"` - Close with reason
- **Tip**: When creating multiple issues/tasks/epics, use parallel subagents for efficiency
- **WARNING**: Do NOT use `bd edit` - it opens $EDITOR (vim/nano) which blocks agents

### Dependencies & Blocking
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` - Add dependency (issue depends on depends-on)
- `bd blocked` - Show all blocked issues
- `bd show <id>` - See what's blocking/blocked by this issue

### Sync & Collaboration
- `bd search <query>` - Search issues by keyword

### Project Health
- `bd stats` - Project statistics (open/closed/blocked counts)
- `bd doctor` - Check for issues (sync problems, missing hooks)
- `bd doctor --check=conventions` - Check for convention drift (lint, stale, orphans)

### Quality Tools
- `bd create --validate` - Check description has required sections
- `bd create --acceptance="criteria"` - Set acceptance criteria (checked by --validate)
- `bd create --design="decisions"` - Record design decisions
- `bd create --notes="context"` - Add supplementary notes
- `bd config set validation.on-create warn` - Auto-validate on every create
- `bd lint` - Check existing issues for missing sections

### Lifecycle & Hygiene
- `bd defer <id> --until="date"` - Defer work to a future date
- `bd supersede <id> --with=<new-id>` - Mark issue as superseded
- `bd close <id> --suggest-next` - Show newly unblocked issues after closing
- `bd stale` - Find issues with no recent activity
- `bd orphans` - Find issues with broken dependencies
- `bd preflight` - Pre-PR checks (lint, stale, orphans)
- `bd human <id>` - Flag for human decision (list/respond/dismiss)

### Structured Workflows
- `bd formula list` - See available workflow templates
- `bd mol pour <name>` - Start structured workflow from formula

## Common Workflows

**Starting work:**
```bash
bd ready           # Find available work
bd show <id>       # Review issue details
bd update <id> --claim  # Claim it
```

**Completing work:**
```bash
bd close <id1> <id2> ...    # Close all completed issues at once
git status                  # Check changed files
# Conservative/minimal/default: report status and proposed commands; wait for approval
# Team-maintainer opt-in only, unless current instructions forbid it:
# git add . && git commit -m "..."
# git push
```

**Creating dependent work:**
```bash
# Run bd create commands in parallel (use subagents for many items)
bd create --title="Implement feature X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=feature
bd create --title="Write tests for X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task
bd dep add beads-yyy beads-xxx  # Tests depend on Feature (Feature blocks tests)
```

> **Note**: AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md are independent files (not symlinked and not sharing an inode). Mirror substantive edits across both, or symlink one to the other.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Generate k82.json from spec/ref-0.9.json with no interpreter imports. Run with python3 -I; --check compares bytes.

Independent of every expander: it sweeps the catalog for its named interfaces and
invoke edges, and expands the adapter edges itself to digests a consumer must match.
"""
import hashlib, json, pathlib, re, sys

HERE = pathlib.Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = HERE.parents[2]
PIN = "5de4e4995350313e50217880ba1b95de39cd043283b726337d4894efb005d197"
raw = (ROOT / "spec/ref-0.9.json").read_bytes()
envelope = b"isa=0.9\nexpansion=ref\nversion=0.9\n" + raw + b"\n"
digest = hashlib.sha256(envelope).hexdigest()
assert digest == PIN, digest
RECIPES = {r["kind"]: r for r in json.loads(raw)["recipes"]}
assert len(RECIPES) == 219
OPEN = sorted(k for k, r in RECIPES.items() if all(isinstance(p, str) for p in r["parameters"]))
SUB = re.compile(r"^([^\[]+)\[(.*)\]$", re.S)
OPS = {"+": lambda a, b: a + b, "-": lambda a, b: a - b, "*": lambda a, b: a * b,
       "div": lambda a, b: a // b, "mod": lambda a, b: a % b, "bit": lambda a, b: (a >> b) & 1}


def scalar(x, ints):
    if isinstance(x, int):
        return x
    if isinstance(x, str):
        return ints[x]
    return OPS[x[0]](*[scalar(y, ints) for y in x[1:]])


def split(w):
    m = SUB.match(w)
    return (m.group(1), json.loads(m.group(2))) if m else (w, None)


def interface(kind):
    """Names used before any binding in body order; open recipes hold only these constructors."""
    used = []

    def go(body, bound):
        for c in body:
            if "gate" in c:
                for w in c["wires"]:
                    n, _ = split(w)
                    if n not in bound and n not in used:
                        used.append(n)
            elif "alloc" in c:
                bound.add(c["alloc"])
            elif "release" in c:
                assert c["release"] in bound, (kind, c)
            elif "for" in c:
                go(c["body"], bound | {c["for"]})
            elif "inverse" in c:
                go(c["body"], bound)
            else:
                raise AssertionError(f"open recipe {kind}: unexpected constructor {sorted(c)}")

    ints = {p for p in RECIPES[kind]["parameters"]}
    go(RECIPES[kind]["body"], set(ints))
    return sorted(used)


def invokes(j):
    if isinstance(j, dict):
        if "invoke" in j:
            yield j
        for v in j.values():
            yield from invokes(v)
    elif isinstance(j, list):
        for v in j:
            yield from invokes(v)


def expand(kind, args):
    """Expand a catalog typed adapter over for/gate/alloc/release/inverse/invoke. Alloc identities are #k in order."""
    out = []
    serial = [0]

    def emit(gate, wires):
        out.append([gate, wires])

    def wire(w, env):
        n, sub = split(w)
        lst = env["wires"][n]
        if sub is None:
            return list(lst)
        i = scalar(sub, env["ints"])
        assert 0 <= i < len(lst), (kind, w, i, len(lst))
        return [lst[i]]

    def declare(body, table):
        for c in body:
            if "alloc" in c:
                table[c["alloc"]] = c
            if "body" in c:
                declare(c["body"], table)

    def acquire(name, width, env):
        ident = serial[0]
        serial[0] += 1
        env["wires"][name] = [f"#{ident}[{i}]" for i in range(width)]
        env["alloc"][name] = f"#{ident}"
        emit("alloc", [f"#{ident}"])

    def walk(body, env, rev):
        """K19 inverse: traverse constructors in reverse, exchanging allocation and release."""
        declare(body, env["decl"])
        for c in reversed(body) if rev else body:
            if "gate" in c:
                assert c["gate"] in ("x", "cx", "ccx", "swap"), c
                emit(c["gate"], [x for w in c["wires"] for x in wire(w, env)])
            elif "alloc" in c or "release" in c:
                name = c.get("alloc", c.get("release"))
                if ("alloc" in c) != rev:
                    acquire(name, scalar(env["decl"][name]["width"], env["ints"]), env)
                else:
                    env["wires"].pop(name)
                    emit("release", [env["alloc"].pop(name)])
            elif "for" in c:
                span = range(scalar(c["from"], env["ints"]), scalar(c["to"], env["ints"]))
                for i in reversed(span) if rev else span:
                    env["ints"][c["for"]] = i
                    walk(c["body"], env, rev)
                env["ints"].pop(c["for"], None)
            elif "inverse" in c:
                walk(c["body"], env, not rev)
            elif "invoke" in c:
                callee = RECIPES[c["invoke"]]
                ps = callee["parameters"]
                assert all(isinstance(p, str) for p in ps), "this expander covers adapter-to-open edges only"
                ints = {p: scalar(a, env["ints"]) for p, a in zip(ps, c["args"], strict=True)}
                # K82: the interface resolves in the invoking scope; nothing else is imported.
                wires = {n: env["wires"][n] for n in interface(c["invoke"])}
                walk(callee["body"], {"ints": ints, "wires": wires, "alloc": {}, "decl": {}}, rev)
            else:
                raise AssertionError(sorted(c))

    r = RECIPES[kind]
    ints, wires = {}, {}
    for p, a in zip(r["parameters"], args, strict=True):
        if p["type"] == "integer":
            ints[p["name"]] = a
        else:
            assert p["type"] == "wire", p
            wires[p["name"]] = [f"{a}[{i}]" for i in range(scalar(p["width"], ints))]
    walk(r["body"], {"ints": ints, "wires": wires, "alloc": {}, "decl": {}}, False)
    return out


def formal_width(p, ints):
    return scalar(p["width"], ints)


def tuples(kind):
    r = RECIPES[kind]
    names = [p["name"] for p in r["parameters"] if p["type"] == "integer"]
    if names == ["N"]:
        return [{"N": n} for n in (1, 2, 3, 4)]
    assert names == ["N", "M"], names
    op = kind.split(".")[-1]
    # §3.3: zext/sext strictly widen, trunc strictly narrows.
    return [{"N": n, "M": m} for n in range(1, 5) for m in range(1, 5) if (m < n if op == "trunc" else n < m)]


edges = []
for caller, r in sorted(RECIPES.items()):
    for site in invokes(r["body"]):
        callee = site["invoke"]
        if callee not in OPEN:
            continue
        assert caller not in OPEN, (caller, callee)
        formals = {p["name"]: p for p in r["parameters"]}
        names = interface(callee)
        for n in names:
            assert formals[n]["type"] == "wire", (caller, n)
        cases = []
        for t in tuples(caller):
            args = [t[p["name"]] if p["type"] == "integer" else f"r_{p['name']}" for p in r["parameters"]]
            leaves = expand(caller, args)
            text = json.dumps(leaves, separators=(",", ":"), ensure_ascii=False).encode()
            cases.append({"args": args,
                          "bundles": {f"r_{p['name']}": formal_width(p, t) for p in r["parameters"] if p["type"] == "wire"},
                          "leaves": len(leaves),
                          "sha256": hashlib.sha256(text).hexdigest()})
        edges.append({"caller": caller, "callee": callee, "interface": names, "instances": cases})
assert len(edges) == 20, len(edges)
invoked = {e["callee"] for e in edges}


def rec(kind, parameters, body):
    return {"kind": kind, "parameters": parameters, "body": body, "scratch": [], "removable": [], "source": "K82 vector"}


def x(w):
    return {"gate": "x", "wires": [w]}


def each_bit(name, n="N"):
    return {"for": "i", "from": 0, "to": n, "body": [x(f'{name}["i"]')]}


I = {"name": "N", "type": "integer"}


def W(name, width):
    return {"name": name, "type": "wire", "width": width}


OPEN_Q = rec("open.q", ["N"], [each_bit("q")])

use bennett_isa_conformance::{json::{self, Json}, ref_language::{Expander, Leaf, Registry}};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
fn sha(s:&str)->String{bennett_isa_conformance::ref_hash::sha256(s.as_bytes())}
fn norm(leaves:&[Leaf])->Vec<(String,Vec<String>)>{
    let mut ids=BTreeMap::new();
    let mut name=|n:&str|->String{ if n.starts_with('@'){let k=ids.len();format!("#{}",*ids.entry(n.to_string()).or_insert(k))}else{n.to_string()}};
    leaves.iter().map(|l|{
        if let Some((n,_,_,_))=&l.allocation{ if l.gate=="alloc"||l.gate=="release"{ return (l.gate.clone(),vec![name(n)]);} }
        (l.gate.clone(),l.wires.iter().map(|(n,i)|format!("{}[{}]",name(n),i)).collect())
    }).collect()
}
fn render(v:&[(String,Vec<String>)])->String{
    let x:Vec<String>=v.iter().map(|(g,w)|{let ws:Vec<String>=w.iter().map(|s|format!("\"{s}\"")).collect();format!("[\"{g}\",[{}]]",ws.join(","))}).collect();
    format!("[{}]",x.join(","))
}
fn main(){
    let t=std::fs::read_to_string(std::env::args().nth(1).unwrap()).unwrap();
    let catalog=std::fs::read_to_string(std::env::args().nth(2).unwrap()).unwrap();
    let v=json::parse(&t).unwrap();
    let (mut ok,mut bad)=(0,0);
    let mut full=Expander::new(&catalog,Registry::new()).unwrap();
    for e in v.get("edges").unwrap().as_arr().unwrap(){
        let caller=e.get("caller").unwrap().as_str().unwrap();
        for c in e.get("instances").unwrap().as_arr().unwrap(){
            let mut bundles:Vec<(String,usize)>=vec![];
            if let Json::Obj(m)=c.get("bundles").unwrap(){for(k,w) in m.iter(){ if let Json::Num(n)=w{bundles.push((k.clone(),*n as usize));}}}
            let b:Vec<(&str,usize)>=bundles.iter().map(|(a,n)|(a.as_str(),*n)).collect();
            let r=full.expand(caller,c.get("args").unwrap().as_arr().unwrap(),&b);
            match r{
                Ok(l)=>{let n=norm(&l);let got=sha(&render(&n));
                    if got==c.get("sha256").unwrap().as_str().unwrap(){ok+=1}else{bad+=1;println!("EDGE DIFF {caller} {} leaves={} want={}",c.get("args").unwrap(),n.len(),c.get("leaves").unwrap());}}
                Err(x)=>{bad+=1;println!("EDGE ERR {caller} {x}");}
            }
        }
    }
    println!("edges: {ok} match, {bad} differ");
    for c in v.get("cases").unwrap().as_arr().unwrap(){
        let id=c.get("id").unwrap().as_str().unwrap();
        let doc=format!(r#"{{"isa":"0.9","name":"ref","version":"0.9","recipes":{}}}"#,c.get("recipes").unwrap());
        let mut bundles:Vec<(String,usize)>=vec![];
        if let Json::Obj(m)=c.get("bundles").unwrap(){for(k,w) in m.iter(){ if let Json::Num(n)=w{bundles.push((k.clone(),*n as usize));}}}
        let b:Vec<(&str,usize)>=bundles.iter().map(|(a,n)|(a.as_str(),*n)).collect();
        let got=Expander::new(&doc,Registry::new()).and_then(|mut e|e.expand(c.get("root").unwrap().as_str().unwrap(),c.get("args").unwrap().as_arr().unwrap(),&b));
        let want=c.get("expect").unwrap();
        let verdict=match(&got,want){
            (Err(x),Json::Str(_))=>format!("PASS rejected: {x}"),
            (Ok(l),Json::Str(_))=>format!("FAIL accepted {}",render(&norm(l))),
            (Err(x),_)=>format!("FAIL rejected: {x}"),
            (Ok(l),w)=>{let g=render(&norm(l));let w=w.to_string().replace(' ',"");if g==w{"PASS".into()}else{format!("FAIL got {g} want {w}")}}
        };
        println!("{id}: {verdict}");
    }
}

# ref 0.9 constructor catalog — complete under draft 0.9

Published byte-identically as [ref-0.9.json](ref-0.9.json) after council acceptance (2026-10-31); the status below records the draft completion checkpoint.

Status: **complete under draft 0.9 — 219 recipes; publication remains gated
on accepted 0.9**. Every row in the disposition table below is bound.
The complete-library hash/count is recorded once in draft §11.1. No ref/
publication or schedule execution is claimed. Historical checkpoints and
family counts remain in [the progress file](ref-0.9-draft.PROGRESS.md).
No interpreter source was read or copied into the checker.

The document is RFC 8785 canonical UTF-8 without a trailing LF; recipes are
ordered by bytewise kind. Every recipe has kind, ordered parameters, body,
scratch, removable:[], and its motivating ISA source. Typed formals,
constructors, lexical capture, call ordinal spaces and fresh allocation
identities are defined by §11.1 K19–K28. An image actual binds exact data
bytes; empty means absent/default-zero even for non-byte-aligned regions.
Template application binds explicit frame/wire actuals while preserving free
captures. Arguments vectors are tied to one closure hash and capture their
actuals' environment; value formals preserve exact §12.3 integer, ptr, f64
and token encodings. K25–K27 add ordered descriptors, source region roles/shapes/origins and shared allocation declarations.

The generators under conformance/tools/ contain no interpreter imports.
Run ref_catalog.py with python3 -I (the required headless project TMPDIR
environment applies). Python's independent integer-subset JCS canonicalizer
checks the printed qxor witness and hashes the four-line envelope. Rust's
independent canonicalizer and SHA-256 must agree with it. Source/scratch
names, bound images and constructor order are hash-significant.

## Evidence and limits

Rust integer/permutation and reduced ordered BDD checkers independently
compare full-domain Boolean functions to plain arithmetic/range/dispatch
semantics, check every scratch boundary, preserve required inputs and
restore the complete state by reverse execution. BDD domain restrictions
(e.g. atop≤capacity, pc≤HALT) and exact tuple exponents are in progress;
these are exhaustive symbolic domains, not random samples. No table-only
helper is called a complete activation/oracle witness. Classical event
witnesses check exact binding bytes, not classical execution or a unitary.

Structured loops use bounds 0,1,2,3,17 at widths 1–4, including nested
finite actual bodies to expansion fixpoint. New step checks cover 1,218,560
tuples across widths 1–4 and row counts 0/1/2/3/17, including heterogeneous
assignment, fault and direct rows plus idle frames. Repeated frame slices
at bounds 0/1/2/3/17 add 3,400 tuples. New qregion scopes cover cell widths

    fn bind(&self, r: &Json, args: &[Json], caller: &Context) -> Result<Context, String> {
        self.bind_seeded(r, args, caller, &Env::new())
    }
    fn bind_seeded(
        &self,
        r: &Json,
        args: &[Json],
        caller: &Context,
        seed: &Env,
    ) -> Result<Context, String> {
        let ps = a(f(r, "parameters")?)?;
        if ps.len() != args.len() {
            return Err("parameter arity".into());
        }
        let mut c = Context {
            ints: seed.clone(),
            controls: caller.controls.clone(),
            source_owner:caller.source_owner.clone(),source_point:caller.source_point.clone(),source_edges:caller.source_edges.clone(),source_steps:caller.source_steps.clone(),source_location:caller.source_location.clone(),
            legacy: ps.iter().all(|p| matches!(p, Json::Str(_))),
            ..Context::default()
        };
        if let Some(names) = r.get("scratch") {
            for name in a(names)? {
                c.scratch_names.insert(s(name)?.into());
            }
        }
        validate_formals(ps)?;
        for (p, v) in ps.iter().zip(args) {
            let (name, ty) = if let Json::Str(n) = p {
                (n.as_str(), "integer")
            } else {
                (s(f(p, "name")?)?, s(f(p, "type")?)?)
            };
            match ty {
                "class" => {
                    c.classes.insert(name.into(), class(v, caller)?);
                }
                "allocation" => {
                    c.handles.insert(
                        name.into(),
                        caller
                            .handles
                            .get(s(v)?)
                            .cloned()
                            .ok_or("unbound allocation declaration")?,
                    c.bodies.insert(name.into(), b);
                }
                "symbol" => {
                    let n = s(v)?;
                    let h = caller.symbols.get(n).map(String::as_str).unwrap_or(n);
                    if h.len() != 64
                        || !h
                            .bytes()
                            .all(|b| b.is_ascii_digit() || (b'a'..=b'f').contains(&b))
                    {
                        return Err("symbol needs closure hash".into());
                    }
                    if !self.registry.contains_key(h) {
                        return Err("unbound closure hash".into());
                    }
                    c.symbols.insert(name.into(), h.into());
                    if let Some(context)=caller.sources.get(n) {c.sources.insert(name.into(),context.clone());}
                }
                "event" => {
                    let event = if let Json::Str(n) = v {
                        caller.events.get(n).cloned().ok_or("unbound event")?
                    } else {
                        v.clone()
                    };
                    keys(&event, &["instruction", "point"])?;
                    s(f(&event, "instruction")?)?;
                    s(f(&event, "point")?)?;
                    c.events.insert(name.into(), event);
                }
                _ => return Err(format!("unknown formal type {ty}")),
            }
        }
        if c.legacy {
            for (n, ws) in &caller.wires {
                c.wires.entry(n.clone()).or_insert_with(|| ws.clone());
            }
        }
        Ok(c)
    }

fn wires(j: &Json, c: &Context) -> Result<Vec<Wire>, String> {
    match j {
        Json::Arr(v) => {
            let mut out = Vec::new();
            for w in v {
                out.extend(wires(w, c)?)
            }
            Ok(out)
        }
        Json::Str(v) => {
            if c.wires.contains_key(v) || c.handles.contains_key(v) {
                return named_wires(v, c);
            }
            let (n, i) = index(v, &c.ints)?;
            let ws = named_wires(&n, c)?;
            Ok(vec![ws.get(i).ok_or("wire index out of range")?.clone()])
        }
        Json::Obj(_) => {
            keys(j, &["range", "from", "to"])?;
            let name = s(f(j, "range")?)?;
            let from =
                usize::try_from(scalar(f(j, "from")?, &c.ints)?).map_err(|_| "negative range")?;
            let to =
                usize::try_from(scalar(f(j, "to")?, &c.ints)?).map_err(|_| "negative range")?;
            let ws = named_wires(name, c)?;
            Ok(ws.get(from..to).ok_or("wire range out of bounds")?.to_vec())
        }
        _ => Err("invalid wire actual".into()),
    }
}
fn validate_formals(ps: &[Json]) -> Result<(), String> {
    let mut names = BTreeSet::new();
    for p in ps {
        let name = if let Json::Str(n) = p {
            n.as_str()
        } else {
            let ty = s(f(p, "type")?)?;
            keys(
                p,
                match ty {
                    "wire" => &["name", "type", "width"],
                    "records" => &["name", "type", "fields", "order"],
                    "region" => &["name", "type", "address", "cell", "role"],
                    "class" | "allocation" => &["name", "type"],
                    "templates" => &["name", "type", "length"],
                    "arguments" => &["name", "type", "symbol"],
                    "value" => &["name", "type", "isa_type"],
                    "integer" | "angle" | "body" | "symbol" | "environment" | "event" | "image" => {
                        &["name", "type"]
                    }
                    _ => return Err("unknown formal type".into()),
                },
            )?;
            if ty == "records" {
                let fields = a(f(p, "fields")?)?;
                validate_formals(fields)?;
                for field in fields {
                    let ty = field
                        .get("type")
                        .and_then(Json::as_str)
                        .unwrap_or("integer");
                    if !matches!(
                        ty,
                        "integer" | "wire" | "region" | "class" | "allocation" | "image"
